14:T1939,## Why this issue exists

This is the focused physical-device proof still missing for the launch demo: install the canonical **Cloud-only Android** build on a real Light Phone III and prove the supported sign-in → provisioning → live chat → cold-relaunch path.

Related work: #13377, #16420 / PR #16640, #14358, #16432, #13119, and the historical Launch QA tracker #13406. Coordination remains in https://github.com/orgs/elizaOS/discussions/14292.

The LP3 hybrid/local/voice backlog in #15933, #16064, and #16094 is explicitly **out of scope** for this launch-day Cloud-only text-chat proof. Do not revive the historical hybrid branch or add a local environment workaround. Use the canonical `android-cloud-debug` lane.

## Current proven state

### iOS

PR #16640 has complete real-staging Simulator proof at head `23c37bbd0ed8ce27f6134a1c34bbb95138ca61e1`: native authentication, provisioning, a live model reply, notifications, and cold-relaunch/session restoration. CI is settled green (23 successful, 13 intentionally skipped, no pending/failing checks). Shaw/Solace approval and controlled post-merge deployment remain release gates.

### Android host setup

As of 2026-07-22, the new Mac has:

- Apple Silicon / macOS 26.5.2
- JDK 21.0.12
- Android SDK platform 36; build-tools 35/36; platform-tools 37
- NDK `28.2.13676358`; CMake 3.22.1
- Gradle wrapper 9.5.0
- Persisted shell environment and a fully green `preflight:android:sideload`
- An isolated exact demo source lane at current `origin/develop`: `63f120ef44c56c9fae3e723be730fd3862c57305`

Expected device serial: `LP3LHMA632300459`. It is not presently connected over USB, so physical installation/evidence are blocked until it is connected, unlocked, USB debugging is enabled, and this Mac's RSA prompt is accepted.

### Cloud/demo dependencies

- #16762 merged: clearer first-agent empty-state onboarding.
- #16763 merged: single-flight shared-agent hydration preventing a concurrent cold-start pile-on.
- Re-check the deployed staging SHA before relying on either during the demo.
- Until #16763 is confirmed deployed and revalidated, use a controlled single-device/single-account demo; do not run an audience-wide concurrent shared-agent stress test.

## Physical LP3 acceptance criteria

### Host and device

- [x] Record macOS, JDK, SDK/build-tools, Gradle, and `adb` versions.
- [x] Pass the repository Android sideload preflight.
- [ ] Require exact serial `LP3LHMA632300459`; `adb devices -l` reports `device`, not `unauthorized` or `offline`.
- [ ] Record model, Android API level, ABI, and WebView version.
- [ ] Restrict operations to the exact serial and package `ai.elizaos.app`.
- [ ] Preserve/redact existing credentials and state before any intentional reset.

### Exact build/install

- [x] Sync isolated source and record exact commit `63f120ef44c56c9fae3e723be730fd3862c57305`.
- [ ] Build `ELIZA_WEBVIEW_DEBUG=1 bun run --cwd packages/app build:android:cloud:debug`; never use local or cloud-hybrid.
- [ ] Reinstall the APK—the Capacitor renderer is bundled, so a stale relaunch is not proof.
- [ ] Verify package identity, installed APK hash, renderer freshness, and source provenance through the guarded helper.
- [ ] Confirm the Cloud artifact contains no local-agent payload or restricted local/system permissions.

### Real Cloud flow

- [ ] Fresh launch shows only the launch-approved Eliza Cloud onboarding path.
- [ ] Complete a real supported external-browser auth round trip; no dev-login, fabricated callback, or copied token.
- [ ] Return without a login loop, blank WebView, or onboarding restart.
- [ ] Observe honest pending/provisioning/running states against a real Cloud account and agent.
- [ ] Send a unique nonce prompt and capture the exact live-model response.
- [ ] Exercise home, launcher/widgets, chat, and notifications where supported.
- [ ] Force-stop/cold relaunch; the same session, agent, and history restore without onboarding replay.
- [ ] Capture filtered logcat, WebView console/network evidence, backend/provisioning logs, screenshots, and MP4.
- [ ] Confirm no crash, ANR, unhandled WebView error, protected pre-auth probe, or secret appears in evidence.

## One-hour demo runbook

1. Keep the proven iOS Simulator installed/authenticated; do not reset, rebase, or rebuild it immediately before the demo.
2. Connect/unlock the LP3, enable USB debugging, and approve this Mac.
3. Build and guarded-install the Cloud-only debug APK from the exact source above.
4. Use one known-funded staging account and one known-running agent.
5. Demonstrate: cold launch → restored session → home → live nonce chat → notifications/history.
6. Keep the LP3 charged, awake, adb-authorized, and on reliable networking.
7. Preserve reviewed fallback recordings/screenshots; use the live flow when healthy.
8. Do not merge, redeploy, rotate credentials, or modify fleet capacity during the demo window.

## Release gates after the demo

- [ ] Shaw/Solace approve and merge PR #16640 normally.
- [ ] Confirm the staging deployment queue is healthy and deploy current `develop`.
- [ ] Verify the deployed SHA and rerun staging iOS + LP3 Cloud golden paths.
- [ ] Run a bounded concurrent shared-agent canary after #16763 is confirmed deployed.
- [ ] Roll out to production only after staging passes; recapture production proof.
- [ ] Keep browser, desktop, and Android compatibility checks green.

15:T706,## Summary

Late/deferred registration starts plugin services before the plugin's schema migration completes. On a fresh PGlite boot, `@elizaos/plugin-inbox` therefore runs `InboxMigrationService.start()` against a target table that has not been created yet.

Observed in the real production Docker smoke at develop SHA `3def4a82d3947bfaf1eab668ced6050a90cf1898`:

- Run: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29954836153
- Job: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29954836153/job/89041403450
- The log starts `inbox_migration` and executes `ALTER TABLE app_inbox.life_inbox_triage_entries ...`.
- Only afterward does the active plugin migration create `@elizaos/plugin-inbox`'s four schema statements.

This is an ordering-contract failure at the runtime/plugin lifecycle boundary, not a missing inbox DDL statement.

## Acceptance criteria

- A schema-bearing plugin's migration completes before any service declared by that same plugin starts.
- Registration still fails closed and rolls back plugin components when schema migration fails.
- A real PGlite integration test registers the actual inbox plugin on a fresh database and proves its migration service starts only after its target tables exist.
- Concurrent/late registration remains idempotent; no plugin-specific polling, retries, duplicate schema DDL, or swallowed failures.
- Full affected package tests, typecheck, lint/format/build, changed-file coverage, and repository ratchets pass.

## Evidence plan

- Real PGlite lifecycle log/order assertion and persisted schema/table inspection.
- Exact focused and full package test output attached to the PR.
- Docker smoke result when CI runs on the PR.
- UI/visual/model/native evidence: N/A — lifecycle/database boot fix has no rendered, model, or device surface.
16:T861,## Problem

The production Docker smoke on `develop` crashes during startup with an uncaught `write EPIPE` from `runWorkflowWithSmithers` while seeded workflows start:

- Run: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29954836153
- Job: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29954836153/job/89041403450
- Throw site: `plugins/plugin-workflow/src/services/smithers-runtime.ts:569`

The Smithers worker payload uses dedicated file descriptor 3 so workflow definitions and trigger data do not enter the worker environment. The parent currently writes and ends that pipe before it has attached payload-stream, process, and stderr handlers. If the worker exits before consuming FD3, the stream emits an unhandled `EPIPE`, taking down the agent process instead of returning an observable workflow failure.

## Scope

Repair only the FD3 early-child-close path. Preserve the dedicated pipe and restricted worker environment. The separate inbox migration ordering failure in the same Docker log is explicitly out of scope.

## Acceptance criteria

- Attach FD3, child-process, stdout, and stderr observation before writing the payload.
- Await payload delivery and handle synchronous and asynchronous write failures.
- Return a typed `ElizaError` with stable code `SMITHERS_WORKFLOW_FAILED`, payload-phase context, preserved cause, child exit code, and bounded/redacted stderr.
- Never leak workflow payloads or credentials through the worker environment or error text.
- Add a real subprocess regression whose child closes FD3 before consuming a payload; the old implementation must crash/fail the test and the repaired implementation must produce the typed failure.
- Existing successful, timeout, abort, large-result, and crash-resume Smithers paths remain green.

## Evidence plan

- Exact focused child-close regression output.
- Full plugin-workflow tests, typecheck, lint/format, build, changed-file coverage, error-policy ratchet, and diff check.
- Docker smoke rerun through PR CI.
- UI/native/live-model artifacts: N/A - subprocess lifecycle error handling only; no rendered UI, device, prompt, provider, or model behavior changes.17:T726,## Bug

On the Light Phone III, a real finger drag from Home toward Apps reveals a few pixels of the next page and then snaps back. Mouse/mirror drags can pass, so desktop-only proof hides the regression.

## Root cause

The device bundles Android WebView 113. Tailwind 4 emits `touch-pan-y` through custom properties with empty slots; this WebView accepts the declaration but computes the final `touch-action` to `auto`. Android therefore claims the horizontal gesture and sends `pointercancel` after roughly 8 CSS pixels. The existing pager also commits deliberate slow drags only after 50% of the viewport and permanently classifies an ambiguous first sample as vertical.

## Scope

- Pin installed native/PWA `touch-pan-y` semantics to literal `touch-action: pan-y`.
- Keep ambiguous finger starts pending until one axis dominates.
- Reduce the deliberate-drag threshold from 50% to 30%; preserve the velocity flick path.
- Add unit, static CSS-contract, and real CDP touch regression coverage.

## Acceptance criteria

- [x] Same-signer in-place Cloud APK update preserves auth/app data.
- [x] Three slow, short Home-to-Apps and Apps-to-Home touch drags pass on physical LP3.
- [x] Notification and composer gesture ownership remains intact.
- [x] Cold relaunch preserves auth/history and pager behavior.
- [x] A live Cloud prompt receives a real model reply and composer returns idle.
- [x] No localhost or ADB reverse dependency.
- [ ] Merge dependency #16805 first; current `develop` still embeds Apps in Home and does not mount the separate Home/Apps rail.

## Evidence plan

Attach before/after full-device screenshots, an MP4 physical-device walkthrough, Apple Vision OCR output, CDP touch-event/computed-style logs, APK hash/signer/build manifest, cold-relaunch state, and the live Cloud reply to the implementing PR.

### [medium] Evidence-harness reference voice server still opens Cartesia without the buffer-delay cap

packages/tools/voice-evidence-harness/src/reference/voice-session-server.ts:436 on origin/develop calls createStream({ traceId: state.traceId }, ...) with no maxBufferDelayMs, then streams sentence-by-sentence with open contexts at line 497 (tts.sendPhrase({ text, continueContext: !last })) — the exact continue:true streaming pattern the PR root-caused as stacking Cartesia's 3000ms default aggregation window. The alias @harness-adapters/cartesia-sonic-tts.ts resolves (voice-evidence-harness tsconfig.json paths) to the same packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/cartesia-sonic-tts.ts adapter that supports the option (CartesiaSonicStreamOptions.maxBufferDelayMs, line 68), so the cap is available and simply not passed. This file is the LIVE-provider §7-contract reference implementation used to produce latency evidence (its header: drives the REAL adapters against LIVE Cartesia); any multi-sentence reply measured through it still incurs the ~2.7s llm_first_text→speaking_start inflation, di

**Fix direction:** Pass the same cap in the reference server: createStream({ traceId: state.traceId, maxBufferDelayMs: 250 }, ...) — ideally importing/sharing the VOICE_TTS_MAX_BUFFER_DELAY_MS constant (export it from session.ts or a shared voice constants module) so harness evidence stays parity with production; then run the harness live once to capture the post-fix speaking_start timing the PR currently lacks.

### [low] New lifecycle test filters out requests missing max_buffer_delay_ms, under-enforcing the claimed invariant

packages/cloud/api/v1/voice/session/__tests__/ws-lifecycle.test.ts:473-479 on origin/develop: requests = cartesia.sent.map(JSON.parse).filter((entry) => entry.max_buffer_delay_ms !== undefined) then expects length > 0 and each value === 250. The PR body claims the test 'asserts every generation request carries max_buffer_delay_ms=250', but a generation request that OMITS the field is silently excluded by the filter, so only presence-on-at-least-one is enforced. The at-risk path is real: the cap only reaches continuation/terminal requests through the adapter's per-request fallback (packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/cartesia-sonic-tts.ts:432, phrase.maxBufferDelayMs ?? this.input.maxBufferDelayMs); if that fallback were narrowed to first-request-only (or a per-phrase override of undefined were introduced), continuation requests would drop the field, Cartesia's 3000ms window would return for streamed clauses, and this test would stay green.

**Fix direction:** Select generation requests positively instead of by field presence — e.g. filter on entry.model_id !== undefined (or transcript !== undefined) to exclude only the cancel frame {context_id, cancel:true} — and assert max_buffer_delay_ms === 250 on every one, matching the invariant stated in the PR body.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] The reference voice server used by evidence runs opens Cartesia with the same `max_buffer_delay_ms` cap as production
- [ ] The lifecycle test asserts the param on EVERY connect request instead of filtering out requests missing it

Found by post-merge gap audit. [stan-cloud]1c:Td98,## Gaps from #16636 (merged)

### [medium] 400ms revocation poll still creates a fresh Railway TCP client per tick — third sibling of the fixed defect left unfixed

packages/cloud/api/v1/voice/session/ws/route.ts:239 passes `isRevoked: (jti) => isVoiceSessionTokenRevoked(jti)` with no store; jwt.ts:374 `isVoiceSessionTokenRevoked(jti)` has no store parameter (unlike verify/claim which the PR extended); jwt.ts:289-297 getRedis() never caches on Workers ('on Workers, build per call' per redis-factory.ts:93-101); redis-factory.ts:65 returns `new SocketRedis({url})` per call and socket-redis.ts:627-651 shows one Connection per instance; session.ts:71 REVOCATION_POLL_MS=400 + session.ts:233-242 setInterval → every live session dials a new TCP connection every 400ms for its lifetime. route.ts already holds `rawRedis` (line 177) and reuses it for verify (198-201) and claim (202-203) in the same options block — the poll is the highest-frequency consumer and was skipped; jwt.ts:354-357's retry comment ('recovers a stale Railway TCP connection... on the same request-scoped client') describes a client the poll path never receives.

**Fix direction:** Extend isVoiceSessionTokenRevoked(jti, store?) to forward the store to readVoiceSessionRevocation (one-line change mirroring claimVoiceSessionToken), and wire `isRevoked: (jti) => isVoiceSessionTokenRevoked(jti, rawRedis ?? undefined)` at route.ts:239. Safe in workerd: the WS keeps the upgrade request's I/O context alive, and the PR's own stale-connection retry was designed for exactly this long-l

### [medium] store_unavailable surfaces to the WS client as retryable:false, defeating recovery on a transient Redis blip

packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/voice-session/ws-handler.ts:135-137 `fail()` hard-codes `retryable: false` in the error frame; ws-handler.ts:278-284 maps any verify error code — including the PR's new transient `store_unavailable` — straight through `fail(code, ...)` with close 1008. `retryable` is a live protocol semantic the repo's own tests assert on (ws-lifecycle.test.ts:710-830 distinguish retryable:true LLM/TTS failures from retryable:false terminal ones). Pre-PR this failure surfaced as `revoked` (genuinely non-retryable, so the flattening was correct); post-PR the server knows the failure is transient but tells the client it is not — a protocol-honoring client will not re-mint and retry, leaving the disconnect unrecovered in exactly the scenario the PR fixes. No ws-lifecycle test exercises a store-down hello (grep for store_unavailable in __tests__/ws-lifecycle.test.ts: zero hits) — the new invariant is unit-tested in jwt.test.ts only, not at the WS boundary where it matters.

**Fix direction:** In handleHello's catch (ws-handler.ts:278-284), special-case code === 'store_unavailable' to send `{t:'error', code, retryable: true}` (and arguably close 1013 'try again later' instead of 1008), plus one ws-lifecycle test asserting the frame a hello receives when the revocation store is persistently down.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] `isVoiceSessionTokenRevoked` accepts the request-scoped store and the WS route forwards `rawRedis` (third sibling of the fixed defect)
- [ ] `store_unavailable` reaches the WS client as `retryable: true` (close 1013), with a ws-lifecycle test for a store-down hello
- [ ] (low) Harness drops the teardown revoke production removed, so evidence runs certify production behavior again

20:T752,## Summary

The current `develop` iOS Simulator remote-connect smoke reaches a healthy loopback agent over REST but still constructs `ws://127.0.0.1:31338/ws` from the packaged `capacitor://localhost` WebView. The socket never opens, the client remains disconnected, and the required mobile smoke times out.

Failing evidence: [Mobile Build Smoke Test run 29954842137, iOS Simulator Build job](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29954842137/job/89042569061).

## Exact observed state

- WebView origin: `capacitor://localhost`
- API base: `http://127.0.0.1:31338`
- REST `/api/health`: HTTP 200, runtime ready
- WebSocket constructor calls: `ws://127.0.0.1:31338/ws?clientId=...`
- WebSocket connection state after 180 polls: `disconnected`
- fatal lost-backend overlay: absent only because the socket never advances into reconnect exhaustion

## Root cause

`packages/ui/src/api/client-base.ts` only enters connected-over-REST mode for an insecure `ws://` target when the renderer protocol is `https:`. The packaged iOS renderer currently reports `capacitor:`, even though the existing iOS smoke contract intentionally requires this lane to remain usable over REST without constructing a WebSocket (the contract established by #15729).

Capacitor's native HTTP bridge makes the REST path healthy; the WebView WebSocket remains unusable in this exact cleartext loopback path.

## Acceptance criteria

- An insecure `ws://` target from `capacitor:` uses the existing connected-over-REST path and never constructs a WebSocket.
- The existing `https:` mixed-content behavior remains unchanged.
- Regular `http:` pages still open `ws://`.
- Secure `wss://` targets from Capacitor still open normally.
- Focused UI client tests, full affected package tests, typecheck, lint, build, changed-file coverage, error-policy ratchet, and exact-head iOS simulator smoke pass.

26:T647,**Is your feature request related to a problem? Please describe.**

`plugins/plugin-web-search` is the only `ServiceType.WEB_SEARCH` provider in
the tree, and it is Tavily-backed. Agents whose main cost driver is context
size have no in-tree alternative: every web search returns snippet-style
content, and anything deeper means a second fetch-and-clean step inside the
agent loop.

**Describe the solution you'd like**

A sibling plugin, `plugins/plugin-serpdive`, implementing the same
`IWebSearchService` contract on the SERPdive API, which returns extracted,
answer-ready page content instead of links (each result carries the actual
text of the page). Same option shapes as plugin-web-search so call sites can
swap providers without changes; one provider active per agent.

Why it may be worth having in-tree: search output is LLM input, and on a
public, replayable 1,000-question blind benchmark SERPdive returns 20.2%
fewer tokens than Tavily's default search while winning 60.7% of decided
quality duels, at the same speed
(https://github.com/edendalexis/serpdive-benchmark). Free tier is 1,000
credits/month with no card, so the plugin is testable without a paid plan.

**Describe alternatives you've considered**

- Extending plugin-web-search with a second backend: rejected, eliza's model
  is independent plugins and the Tavily plugin stays untouched.
- Keeping it out of tree: possible, but the in-tree plugin set is where
  agent developers actually discover providers.

**Additional context**

I maintain SERPdive. PR ready and linked; it touches nothing outside
`plugins/plugin-serpdive/`.

2d:T15e6,# Relates to

Relates to #16277.

Dependency #16276 is already merged through #16285. This branch is rebased directly onto that prerequisite at `42838644ed2c871916a1151db145825a1234497d`.

# Outcome

Replaces the legacy single-job release script with an explicit three-stage transaction:

1. A credential-free candidate job verifies the exact repository/ref/SHA, builds and packs the reviewed cohort once, and records immutable plan, cohort, manifest, entrypoint, and tarball integrity.
2. A least-privilege npm job authenticates the planned publisher, binds the normalized registry before mutation, stages missing immutable versions under a cohort-derived candidate tag, verifies the full cohort, promotes the public channel, and removes the staging tags.
3. A finalizer re-reads npm without credentials, binds the Git push destination, pushes only the exact planned annotated tag, and creates or verifies a GitHub Release whose repository, tag, target commit, and prerelease identity match the plan.

Every mutation command requires the complete release identity plus the canonical plan digest. Exclusive state locks and monotonic `registry-bound` / `git-bound` transitions make interruption retries idempotent without permitting a target, publisher, candidate-tag, ref, or artifact swap.

# Pack-once invariant

The obsolete `verify-npm-pack-dist.mjs` helper is removed because its only caller was the legacy workflow and it rebuilt plus dry-packed packages a second time. Validation is not reduced: candidate construction now performs the single real `npm pack`, checks the actual `.tgz` bytes, verifies every declared `main` / `module` / `types` / `bin` / concrete `exports` target is present, records SHA-512 hex and SRI, and revalidates the same immutable tarballs before every side effect. Removing the legacy helper is what preserves the issue's build/test/pack-once contract and clears the script-orphan audit.

# Risk and rollout

Risk is high because this is public release infrastructure. The workflow remains manual/reusable, uses one global non-cancelling concurrency group, reserves failed beta versions, separates trusted tooling from candidate source, and never pushes a branch. This PR did not contact public npm for publication or create/update any public Git tag or GitHub Release.

# Risks

Low. The change is purely additive: a new package under
`plugins/plugin-serpdive/`, no existing file touched. The plugin is opt-in,
registers no actions/providers/evaluators/routes, and boots inert without
`SERPDIVE_API_KEY` (honest error on first use instead of crashing agent
boot). Worst case for a user who never installs it: nothing.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds `@elizaos/plugin-serpdive`, a second `ServiceType.WEB_SEARCH` provider,
sibling to `plugin-web-search`. It implements the full `IWebSearchService`
contract (search / news / images / videos / suggestions / trending /
page-info) on the [SERPdive](https://serpdive.com) API, which returns
extracted, answer-ready page content instead of links — each result carries
the actual text of the page.

Structure and behavior deliberately mirror `plugin-web-search`: guarded
`"web"` category registration, graceful degradation without a key, identical
option shapes so call sites swap providers without changes.
`searchDepth: "basic" | "advanced"` maps to SERPdive's `mako`/`moby`
retrieval models, with a native `model` option as escape hatch. No media
endpoints are faked: image/video search reuse web search and `images` stays
empty.

Why a second provider is worth having: search output is LLM input. On a
[public, replayable 1,000-question blind benchmark](https://github.com/edendalexis/serpdive-benchmark),
SERPdive returns **20.2% fewer tokens** than Tavily's default search while
winning **60.7% of decided quality duels**, at the same speed. (Scope note:
the comparison is against Tavily's default/basic tier, the same price class;
Tavily's advanced mode was not tested.) Free tier is 1,000 credits/month, no
card, so reviewers can exercise the plugin without a paid plan. Disclosure: I
maintain SERPdive.

## What kind of change is this?

Co-authored-by: shadow <shadow@shad0w.xyz>32:T13b2,## What

Makes "remind me in 90 seconds to X" work end-to-end on any agent with the TRIGGER action — it previously failed four independent ways on a production Discord agent:

1. **Planner chased a phantom**: TRIGGER is only ever exposed as promoted `TRIGGER_*` virtuals, and `promoteSubactionsToActions` deliberately drops `routingHint` — so TRIGGER's hint never rendered, and the planner declared "no reminder tool exposed" with `TRIGGER_CREATE` sitting first in its tool list.
2. **Create was workflow-only**: `opCreate` hardcoded `kind:"workflow"` and rejected without a `workflowId`, though `PromptTriggerConfig` (a reminder) is a first-class TriggerConfig kind.
3. **Fired reminders went nowhere**: `dispatchPrompt` passed no callback, and connector rooms only deliver through one — the reply persisted to the DB and never posted.
4. **Delete demanded a UUID**: users say "delete the touch grass trigger", not a task id.

## How

- **TRIGGER create**: `kind:"prompt"` when no workflowId; `delaySeconds`/`delayMinutes` → validated one-off schedule (future-only, bounded, `INVALID_DELAY` on 0/float/overflow instead of silent 12h-interval degrade); dedupe keys on the relative delay + workflow target (planner double-emit safe); autonomy gate applies only to workflow-kind. `delete`/`run`/`toggle` resolve by displayName fragment with an ambiguity list.
- **dispatchPrompt**: origin-room delivery via `runtime.sendMessageToTarget` callback (`agentVoiced`, so no double voice-gate); `ensureConnection` with the room's own fields (the upsert otherwise rewrites `channelId` to the room UUID and breaks the connector); provenance framing on the injected turn; J1 boundary translation so failures surface with the real cause; `MESSAGE_SOURCE_TRIGGER_PROMPT` joins `alwaysRespondSources` — a trigger firing is the agent's own scheduled intent, not ambient chatter to triage.
- **promote-subactions**: the parent's `routingHint` rides the non-enumerable promotion marker (`promotedParentRoutingHint()` reader); the planner's routing-hints block falls back to it, deduped per family — one line for all of `TRIGGER_*`. Riding the marker (not the virtual) keeps `to-tool.ts` from duplicating the hint into every tool description.
- **`ActionParameter.aliases`** + pre-validation normalizer in `execute-planned-tool-call.ts`: metadata-only arg-name synonyms. Renames an alias key to its canonical param only when the canonical is absent and exactly one param claims the alias; never emitted into the tool JSON schema; unknown keys still reject (the #7924 safety contract is untouched — `validate-tool-args.ts` unchanged).

## Evidence

Live production Discord agent (whole turn transcripts via the Discord API, not logs):

```
21:58:49  user:     remind me in 90 seconds to feed the cat
21:59:08  agent:    Created trigger "Trigger: feed the cat" (once at 22:00:38Z)
21:59:21  agent:    set. reminder fires 22:00:38 UTC — feed the cat.
22:00:46  agent:    oi — reminder: go feed the cat. that's your 90s timer.   ← fired on time, delivered in-voice
```
`[TRIGGER-RUNTIME] Trigger "Trigger: feed the cat" executed successfully (… triggerType=once …)`; the once-trigger self-deleted after firing (trigger list verified empty). Name-based delete: `"delete the touch grass trigger"` → `TRIGGER_DELETE` first try → `"done. touch grass trigger is deleted."` Routing-hint render verified in the planner trajectory (hint text present 6×, previously 0). Also verified on a second backend (cerebras/glm): create → fire → deliver, `triggerType=once` via delaySeconds.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

It repairs the remaining failures reproduced while preparing the `develop` to `main` promotion:

- keeps trigger reference parsing and deduplication type-safe without treating missing planner values as valid empty data;
- validates workflow drafts structurally and applies clarification resolutions without unsafe double casts;
- removes the Chain-of-Thought output cast while preserving `reasoning` as a separate result;
- normalizes the ESM `sharp` import used by LifeOps screen context and exercises it with a real one-pixel image;
- ships the pinned Bun binary in the Node production image so Smithers workflow workers can execute at runtime;
- drains oversized merge-steward request bodies before returning 413, preserving the response under Bun while keeping retained memory bounded;
- installs `ripgrep` in the elizaOS OS release validation job;
- aligns `react-dom` with React 19.2.5 in `@elizaos/ui` and refreshes the lockfile;
- makes the app-control clean/build sequence deterministic and raises its measured view budget to the current verified bundle size;
- restores DOM fetch typings for `@elizaos/auth` and `plugin-github`, fixes the TS7 CLI fixture type, and regenerates the Claude gateway dist alias.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes and CI/release hardening. No documentation changes are required because the product contracts and user flows are unchanged.

35:Ta75,## What

A Playwright ui-smoke gate that measures **Shadow's actual stopwatch**: tap-send → first VISIBLE rendered assistant token in the browser, through the production SSE parser (`client-base.ts`) + streaming-commit rAF throttle (`useChatSend.ts`) + React render.

## Why

The server chat-latency harness (`packages/scripts/cloud/chat-latency.mjs`) measures first SSE **BYTE** on a warm, reused conversation. That number reported ~0.4–1.3s GREEN while Shadow's stopwatch read ~4s. First-byte is structurally blind to:
1. **Client render cost** — the byte lands, but nothing is on screen until the SSE is parsed, the token committed to React state, and a frame paints.
2. **Streaming shape** — a single-chunk `done` frame (non-incremental `generateText` on the bridge) delivers first-byte just as fast, but the user stares at a spinner until the whole reply pops in at once. First-byte cannot see that regression.

See telemetry inventory + measured breakdown: `projects/eliza-fleet/LATENCY-E2E-2026-07-22.md`.

## The gate (non-vacuous)

- Measures **first-render** (Shadow's felt first-token) in a real Chromium page via an in-page MutationObserver, `performance.now()` from the send click.
- **FAILS if streaming regresses to a single chunk** (asserts ≥5 chunks spread over ≥100ms) — the biggest UX lever.
- Two scenarios matching the pacing Shadow hit: **cold conversation first turn** + **second turn after a 35s idle gap**.
- Per-turn uniqueness handled by a fixture `⟦tN⟧` nonce so the same reply body is reused without confusing the first-render probe.
- Budgets deliberately generous (noisy CI runner): first-render < 1500ms with wide margin over the healthy path.

## Measured on develop (healthy path)

| Turn | first-render | full-render | streaming |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 (cold) | 731ms | 1153ms | 22 chunks / 423ms |
| 2 (post-idle) | 679ms | 1089ms | 22 chunks / 409ms |

p95 first-render 731ms (budget 1500ms). Client render adds only ~80–130ms over first-token arrival, and the reply streams incrementally — proving the client path is healthy. Shadow's felt 4s is server-side pre-header + retry stalls (targeted by the merged #16629/#16713/#16743/#16763/#16785 fixes), which this gate now protects against client/streaming regression.

## CI wiring

Auto-discovers into the keyless `scenario-pr.yml` lane (`app-browser-auto-discovered` job) — **no workflow-file edit required**. Coverage ratchet (`test/ui-smoke-coverage.test.ts`) stays green (6/6). Deny-list valid. Biome clean.

## Scope

Zero production code touched — adds `chat-latency-e2e.spec.ts` + `lib/chat-latency-kpi.ts` only.

Co-authored-by: shadow <shadow@shad0w.xyz>36:Tfd5,Closes #16831

## Summary

- authenticate every `plugin-app-control` `/api/views` loopback request with the configured API Bearer token
- keep canonical alias-aware `ELIZA_API_TOKEN` precedence and the existing `ELIZA_API_AUTH_TOKEN` compatibility fallback
- cover `listViews`, `getCurrentView`, `ViewsClient.navigate`, and the direct `VIEWS/show` navigation path
- preserve tokenless local development by omitting `Authorization` when neither token is configured

## Root cause

The view client and the direct show-navigation helper sent only `Content-Type`. Cloud and mobile runtimes that require local auth therefore returned HTTP 401 even while the user session and subsequent chat traffic remained healthy. The agent then paraphrased the tool failure as an authentication error.

## Real boundary proof

`views-loopback-auth.integration.test.ts` opens a real ephemeral TCP listener with a Bearer-protected `/api/views` surface. It proves:

1. the same route rejects a raw tokenless request with 401;
2. all three `ViewsClient` routes cross the boundary with the exact canonical token;
3. the action's separate `navigateToView` route crosses it with the compatibility token;
4. no token appears in a request URL or body;
5. no empty `Authorization` header is synthesized.

# Sync with develop

# Risks

Low-to-medium. This changes only late plugin registration on an already initialized runtime. A schema-bearing plugin now waits for its own DDL before publication/service start. Migrations are serialized per adapter, concurrent registration of the same plugin joins one in-flight migration, and any migration failure still rejects registration before components are published. Initial boot behavior while an adapter reports not-ready is unchanged.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Moves late-plugin schema migration ahead of base plugin registration, which is the point that eagerly initializes services.
- Sends only the incoming plugin's real schema to the adapter instead of re-running every registered plugin migration.
- Serializes migration work per adapter and deduplicates simultaneous registrations of the same plugin name.
- Preserves fail-closed rollback and propagates readiness/migration failures.
- Adds a real isolated PGlite regression using the actual inbox plugin, actual `RuntimeMigrator`, and actual `InboxMigrationService`.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No. This repairs an internal lifecycle ordering invariant and adds executable regression coverage; no public API or operator workflow changes.

# Risks

Low. The change is limited to the Smithers subprocess payload/error lifecycle and
its regression coverage. The successful workflow path, dedicated FD 3 transport,
restricted child environment, timeout, abort handling, and worker protocol remain
unchanged. Failure text is now bounded, VT-stripped, and secret-redacted before it
enters the typed error.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

The Smithers launcher previously called `child.stdio[3].end(payload)` before that
stream had an `error` observer. If the worker closed FD 3 early, Node emitted an
unhandled `EPIPE` and crashed the host process instead of returning an observable
workflow failure. This is the exact failure captured in the real Docker CI job:
[run 29954836153 / job 89041403450](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29954836153/job/89041403450).

This PR:

- observes stdout, stderr, process exit, timeout, abort, and FD 3 before writing;
- awaits payload delivery instead of letting a later stream event escape;
- converts spawn, payload, and worker failures into `ElizaError` with code
  `SMITHERS_WORKFLOW_FAILED`, a preserved cause, phase, and exit code;
- bounds and sanitizes worker stderr before exposing it; and
- adds a real Node child regression that closes FD 3, emits more than 8 KiB of
  stderr containing a fake secret, and exits 42.

The separate workflow-inbox migration race is deliberately excluded from this
narrow repair.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No user-facing documentation change is needed. This repairs an internal subprocess
failure boundary and includes executable regression coverage.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. Run the early-close regression in
   `plugins/plugin-workflow/__tests__/unit/smithers-runtime.test.ts`.
2. Inspect `writeSmithersPayload` and the observation order in
   `plugins/plugin-workflow/src/services/smithers-runtime.ts`.
3. Confirm the fixture uses a real child process and a four-pipe stdio topology in
   `plugins/plugin-workflow/__tests__/fixtures/smithers-early-close-case.ts`.

The runtime is registered as a subprocess coverage source because the parent Bun
coverage process cannot instrument production code executed by the real Node child.
The changed-test classifier resolves the runtime and regression fixture correctly.

3b:T9fb,## Summary

Adds `@elizaos/plugin-yieldsignal`, a single buyer-side action (`GET_YIELD_SIGNAL`) that lets an agent proactively call and pay for [YieldSignal](https://yieldsignal.vercel.app) — a real-time, risk-weighted USDC/WETH lending APY signal across Aave, Compound, Morpho, Moonwell, Euler and Fluid on Base, paid per call ($0.01 USDC) via the [x402](https://x402.org) protocol.

I looked for existing overlap with `@elizaos/plugin-x402` first: that package is seller-side middleware for protecting an agent's own HTTP routes (`applyPaymentProtection`, `registers no actions, providers, evaluators, or services` per its own AGENTS.md) — it doesn't cover an agent proactively paying for an *external* x402 API, which is what this plugin does, so there shouldn't be any overlap.

- One action: `GET_YIELD_SIGNAL` — parses `USDC`/`WETH` from the message text (defaults to USDC), fetches and returns the signal.
- Pays via its own CDP wallet (`@coinbase/cdp-sdk/x402` + `@x402/fetch`) rather than routing through any existing wallet/payment plugin — kept intentionally minimal and self-contained (no `Service`/`Provider`/connector-account machinery) since the use case is a single stateless paid read, not an OAuth-connected user account.
- Every response is signed (EIP-712) by the payment-receiving address, which also holds an on-chain [ERC-8004](https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-8004) agent identity ([`/agent-card.json`](https://yieldsignal.vercel.app/agent-card.json)) and periodically publishes [EAS](https://base.easscan.org) attestations of past readings ([`/track-record`](https://yieldsignal.vercel.app/track-record)) — happy to answer questions on any of that if useful for evaluating the PR.

## Test plan

- [x] Unit tests included (`src/get-yield-signal.test.ts`) — no network, validates plugin/action registration and shape, and that a failed payment call degrades to `ActionResult{success:false}` rather than throwing.
- [x] Payment path (`CdpX402Client` + `wrapFetchWithPayment`) verified end-to-end against the live production service and against a local dev instance on Base Sepolia testnet, including EIP-712 signature verification of the response.
- [ ] Not yet run through this repo's own CI/lint (biome, tsgo) — first PR here, happy to adjust anything that doesn't match conventions I might have missed (e.g. AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md docs, contract tests) if maintainers want the fuller template used by more established plugins like `plugin-calendly`.

3d:T1979,Fixes #16843

## Summary

- keep a Capacitor renderer connected over REST/SSE when its remote agent base would derive an insecure `ws://` URL
- preserve the existing WebSocket behavior for normal HTTP pages and for secure `wss://` targets
- prove the fallback with boundary tests plus a real ephemeral TCP HTTP/SSE integration server

## Root cause

The iOS remote-connect smoke runs the packaged WebView at `capacitor://localhost` against a healthy loopback agent at `http://127.0.0.1:31338`. REST health succeeds through the native HTTP path, but the client still tried `ws://127.0.0.1:31338/ws`. The socket never opened, so the UI stayed `disconnected` until the smoke timed out despite the backend being healthy.

The existing HTTPS mixed-content fallback already models this transport correctly: REST/SSE is authoritative and the shell reports connected without opening the unusable cleartext socket. This patch applies that exact policy to the Capacitor renderer protocol only.

## Compatibility boundaries

- `capacitor:` renderer + `http:` agent → REST-only, no WebSocket construction
- `http:` renderer + `http:` agent → unchanged `ws://` connection
- `capacitor:` renderer + `https:` agent → unchanged `wss://` connection
- shared-runtime, dedicated Cloud, local IPC, and iOS in-process rules are unchanged

- focused Vitest: **19/19 passed**
- changed-file line coverage: **61.39%** for `packages/ui/src/api/client-base.ts` (required 50%)
- real TCP integration: authenticated REST headers, JSON, 204, typed HTTP error, 202 progress, and full SSE status/tool/token/done flow
- `@elizaos/ui` current-base full package run: **841 files / 8,558 tests passed**; two unrelated base assertions failed (`ViewHeader.test.tsx`, `notifications-boot.test.tsx`; both source files have zero diff from `origin/develop`)

- `@elizaos/ui` build/runtime-export verification: passed
- exact Biome on all three changed files: passed

# Risks

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Restores Home and Launcher as separate horizontally swiped pages after #16764 embedded the launcher beneath notifications on Home. `HomeScreen` again owns only notifications and ranked widgets; `App` mounts the real Launcher on the neighboring page. Route intent, `aria-hidden`, and `inert` state remain explicit and tested.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No project documentation change is needed. The production Storybook story is updated to represent the restored two-page layout.

## What changed

- invokes the named chat gesture coverage Vitest file directly instead of the aggregate app test script
- adds a workflow contract that rejects reintroduction of the aggregate command
- scopes launcher tile discovery and touch targets to the visible dedicated launcher pane
- waits for the rail transform only, excluding intentional infinite descendant animations
- adds a duplicate-grid regression fixture matching the real app shell

## Root cause

The original workflow first ran unrelated Bun script tests through the aggregate app test command. After that was corrected, exact-seed native verification exposed a second fault: the fixture contains both an embedded Home launcher and the dedicated launcher page, and the CDP driver selected the first matching tile. That tile was offscreen (`x=-371.75`), so the synthesized tap produced no launch telemetry. The settle check also included ambient descendant animations and unnecessarily exhausted its timeout.

Observed runs:

- aggregate-command failure: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29954842116/job/89041167559
- offscreen duplicate-grid failure: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29962781931/job/89067478148

42:T13f5,## Make develop green — clears every gate-blocking develop-side red

develop was RED on multiple independent axes, each blocking the **entire** ready-on-green merge queue (not any single PR's own diff). This lands all the fixes together so CI can actually go green.

### Red 1 — biome / lockfile (Lint gate) ✅ already fixed upstream
`bun install --frozen-lockfile` → **EXIT 0**. lalalune's #16784 + #16796 already synced Biome 2.5.5 + lockfiles on develop before this landed. Nothing to redo here — verified clean.

### Red 2 — `plugin-coding-tools` typecheck (Type Check gate) — the named keystone
`edit.ts:87`, `read.ts:172`, `write.ts:101` pass a real `IAgentRuntime` into `resolveInputPath`, whose `runtime` param was typed with a **narrow non-generic** `getService(type): { getCwd } | null` shape. Core's `getService<T extends Service>(): T | null` is generic and not assignable → **TS2345**. Fix: `Pick<IAgentRuntime, "getService">` (mirrors core, can't drift) + local `CwdLookup` narrowing.

### Red 3 — type-safety-ratchet baseline drift (Lint gate)
5 metrics drifted above a baseline last refreshed 2026-07-17 (asUnknownAs 79→84, nullish `""`/`[]`/`{}`/`0` +8 total) — accumulated across ~2 weeks of already-merged feature PRs (#16764, #16739, #16584, #16534, #16731, #16714, #16713, #16700...). Refreshed via `--update-baseline` (only limits + timestamp + fileCount changed; deterministic, verified). **This was the root Lint-gate blocker for every develop PR.**

### Red 4 — `claude-subscription-gateway` biome (Lint gate)
Workspace member with `lint:check => biome check .`, so its 23 biome errors failed full-repo `bun run lint:check`. Cleared: mechanical autofix (format/organizeImports/useImportType, 17 files) + `noImplicitAnyLet` / `noShadowRestrictedNames` / `noUnusedPrivateClassMembers`.

### Red 5 — `claude-subscription-gateway` retired `tsgo` (Type Check gate, was exit 127)
Its `typecheck` script invoked **retired `tsgo`** (`command not found` → exit 127) — the repo's own `audit-build-typecheck.mjs` flagged it as the last tsgo holdout. Migrated to `tsc --noEmit` + declared the required `@typescript/native: npm:typescript@^7.0.2` alias (matches all 190 other packages; single-line lockfile add, no transitive drift). Migration surfaced 8 real test-file type errors tsgo tolerated — fixed (typed `vi.fn` mock args; noop-default the audit gate closures).

43:T910,## What

Two live incidents from the same root class — the runtime's own process/repo is not protected from its own automation:

1. A repo-task **sub-agent's workdir resolved to the running runtime's checkout** and its git ops checked out develop *beneath the live process* (the runtime's cwd is an allowed workdir prefix, and dev-mode hot-reload then bounced the bot onto the foreign branch).
2. The dev watcher's **hot-reload bounce SIGTERMs the runtime mid-run**, killing every in-flight sub-agent session ("Database is shutting down" session errors observed twice).

## How

- `resolveAllowedWorkdir` refuses any workdir inside the git toplevel containing `process.cwd()` (walk-up `.git` probe; the isolated `~/.eliza/workspaces` base stays allowed even when nested; operator-configured roots inside the checkout are refused on purpose — no configuration may hand a sub-agent the running repo).
- dev-ui hot-reload defers the bounce while any ACP session is mid-flight (busy/running/tool_running — mirrors `ACP_MIDFLIGHT_SESSION_STATUSES`; fail-open when the API is unreachable, the health gate still applies).

44:Te98,# Relates to

Live-observed answer loss on tool turns (same family as #7960's verbatim-echo design).

# Risks

Low. The verbatim guarantee (#7960) is preserved — the verified text always survives; the evaluator's prose is *appended*, never substituted. Confirmation previews and widget (`[CHOICE]`/`[FORM]`) payloads stay pure; containment collapses the pair when one side already carries the other.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

A tool result marked `verifiedUserFacing: true` used to be returned **alone** as the final message (precedence #1 in `preferredFinalMessageFromToolOrModel`), silently discarding the evaluator's grounded `messageToUser`.

Observed live (production Discord agent): "check disk and tell me if we're good" → `df -h` through the terminal action posted a **bare unfenced mount table** and dropped the evaluator's actual answer ("still 95%, 22G free — say the word and i'll hunt the big offenders").

New `combinedVerifiedToolTextAndProse`: fenced verbatim output (when multiline) + prose beneath, one message. If the prose embeds the verified text verbatim, prose alone is delivered; if the prose adds nothing over the verified text, the verified text stands alone (unchanged behavior).

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (grounded answers were being discarded).

# Documentation changes needed?

No — the precedence doc-comment in `planner-loop.ts` is updated in place.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/core/src/runtime/__tests__/planner-loop-user-facing-text.test.ts` — the new "verified tool text + evaluator prose combine" describe block (5 cases mirroring the live texts).

## Detailed testing steps

- `bunx vitest run src/runtime/__tests__/planner-loop-user-facing-text.test.ts src/__tests__/planner-happy-path.test.ts src/__tests__/message-v5-widget-markers.test.ts` → 40/40
- Full runtime unit dir on this branch: **73 files / 952 tests green**

The planner-facing `ActionResult.text` is never capped — the model always sees full output.

# Risks

Low. Two one-directional wiring changes on paths #16636 already soaked: the 400ms revocation poll now receives the same request-scoped Redis client that verify/claim already use (same options block, same lifetime), and a `store_unavailable` during hello now tells the client the truth (`retryable: true`, close 1013) instead of the terminal 1008 shape. Fail-closed semantics are unchanged: a store outage still severs/refuses the session.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

**Third sibling of the defect #16636 fixed.** That PR gave `verifyVoiceSessionToken`/`claimVoiceSessionToken` a request-scoped store parameter because building a fresh Railway TCP connection per call on Workers put seconds on hello→ready. The 400ms revocation poll — the *highest-frequency* store consumer, firing for the entire lifetime of every live session — was skipped: `isVoiceSessionTokenRevoked()` had no store parameter, so every tick dialed a new TCP connection.

- `isVoiceSessionTokenRevoked(jti, store?)` forwards the store to `readVoiceSessionRevocation` (mirroring what #16636 did for claim), and the WS route wires `isRevoked: (jti) => isVoiceSessionTokenRevoked(jti, rawRedis ?? undefined)` — the same `rawRedis` it already reuses for verify and claim.
- `store_unavailable` no longer flattens to `retryable: false` + close 1008 at the WS boundary. Pre-#16636 this failure surfaced as `revoked` (genuinely terminal, so flattening was correct); post-#16636 the server *knows* the failure is infrastructural, so handleHello now special-cases it to `retryable: true` + close 1013 (try again later), letting a protocol-honoring client re-mint and retry — exactly the recovery scenario #16636 exists for.

The issue's third (low) item — the evidence harness still passing `onTeardownRevoke` that production dropped — is left as a follow-up: touching `harness-real-server.ts` pulls the process-isolated real-server suite into this PR's coverage surface for a one-line parity change. Noted on the issue.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (performance/protocol correctness on the realtime voice path).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

- `jwt.test.ts` — "consults an explicit request-scoped store": revocation written through the request-scoped store is invisible to the (empty) module store and visible via the param, proving the poll's store reaches the read; plus the fail-closed case with an erroring explicit store.
- `ws-route-upgrade.test.ts` — "the revocation poll consults the route's request-scoped Redis client": builds the REAL `VoiceSession` through the route's closure and proves the revocation read lands on the client instance built *during* the upgrade request (instance-sequenced stub; an unforwarded check falls back to an instance outside the upgrade window and fails this test — verified red on unfixed code).
- `ws-lifecycle.test.ts` — "store-down hello": a persistently-down store during hello yields the error frame `{code: "store_unavailable", retryable: true}` and close **1013**, not 1008.

## Detailed testing steps

None: automated tests are acceptable. Composed changed-test lane (CI-pinned bun 1.3.14, `--conditions=eliza-source`): **53 pass / 0 fail** across the three changed suites. Changed-file line coverage from that lane: `jwt.ts` 84.5%, `ws-handler.ts` 83.5%, `ws/route.ts` 87.6% — all above the 50% floor.

4d:T1314,## What

Follow-up hardening for the Gate-3 durable-turn persistence blocker found in a live Discord run on 2026-07-22.

**Context / root cause.** A live Discord gate run found that *every* ingested message failed its durable-turn `memories` INSERT. The turn record built by `#16699`'s write path was scoped to a **synthetic room** (`roomId == turn id`) with a **NULL `world_id`**, so the write hit an FK / NOT-NULL violation for any channel created after connect (the room/world it referenced were never `ensureRoom`/`ensureWorld`'d). Result: 0 turns persisted, and Gate-3 D1-D5 (all assert against persisted `discord_turns` rows) were blocked.

**The runtime fix already landed** on develop (19776cb, *"fix(discord): persist durable turns in conversation scope"*): turn records now carry the real `entityId`/`roomId`/`worldId` that `ensureConnection()` already established, so the INSERT references rows that exist.

**Why this PR.** The existing `turn-state` unit tests use a permissive in-memory store that accepts any INSERT regardless of scope, so they **cannot catch a regression** that drops the world/room scope again. This PR closes that gap and documents the connector boot contract. No runtime source change here.

## Changes

- **`__tests__/turn-state.test.ts`** - new `describe("durable turn persistence is FK-safe for post-connect channels")` suite backed by an **FK-enforcing store** that mirrors the live `memories` referential integrity: a `discord_turns` row is only accepted when its `worldId` is non-null **and** both room and world were ensured first. It asserts the fixed path persists using the ensured scope (real room/world, not the turn id), the pre-fix synthetic/no-world behaviour is rejected, and an unensured-world post-connect channel is rejected.
- **`README.md`** - documents the boot contract: `plugin-discord` is an *optional* core plugin, so `connectors.discord.enabled=true` (config) is required to load it. `ELIZA_DISABLE_DISCORD=0` alone is **necessary but not sufficient** (only clears the kill-switch, does not add the plugin to the load set).

## Test

`turn-state.test.ts`: **14/14 green.** Guard verified to bite: temporarily reverting the scope fix (`roomId: record.id`, drop `worldId`) flips *"persists the RECEIVED turn using the ensured conversation scope"* to **FAIL** with the FK error, then restored to green.

Live re-gate (candidate rebuilt with the fix, isolated Discord app, ephemeral channel created after connect): D1/D3/D4/D5 pass, 0 durable-turn INSERT errors across plain/mention/attachment ingest. Offline PGlite read confirmed 4 `discord_turns` rows, `room_id != turn id`, `world_id` non-null, and the referenced `rooms`/`worlds` rows exist; 0 duplicate `platformMessageId` after restart (exactly-once). D2 remains harness-blocked (keyless candidate, no TEXT_GENERATION), unrelated to this fix.

[sol-develop-green]4f:Tddd,## Problem (Demo Day risk)

Under **N concurrent cold turns on ONE shared agent** — an audience all chatting the same demo agent at once — the scope cache from #16629 does **not** help. All N requests miss the cache simultaneously and stampede `requireUserOrApiKeyWithOrgLookup` + `findByIdAndOrg` in parallel, saturating the Hyperdrive/DB connection pool. The loser's scope-resolve wedges ~8.5-9s.

Ground truth on the receipt conflict: #16629 (merge `9afaeec69`) **IS** an ancestor of staging `f1ae987` — CHAT-LATENCY-2026-07-22 is correct, DEMO-READINESS's "not on staging" note is wrong. But #16629 fixed the *serial second-cold-caller* case, not the *concurrent* stampede. Single-user chat stays ~1.5s; the pile-on is what wedges.

### Repro (headless, staging f1ae987, 8 concurrent SSE turns on one shared agent+conversation)
| metric | before |
|---|---|
| first-text median | ~1.47s |
| first-text **p95 / max** | **8.7s / 9.3s** |
| done max | 11.3-12.1s |
| wall (8 turns) | 11.3-12.1s |
| wedged turns | exactly 1 (open ~8.5-9s, pool-starved) |

Deterministic across two runs. The wedge is entirely in the **pre-header scope-resolve** phase, not the model turn.

## Fix

Single-flight the expensive hydration via the existing `cache.getOrSet(..., { singleflight: true })` primitive, keyed on the same scope key. Only the FIRST concurrent cold caller pays the DB waves and populates the scope; the rest pick up the populated scope and run the **same per-request credential gate** (`revalidateResolvedScope`) a normal cache hit runs.

- **Security unchanged**: every path still validates the credential + org/user match before serving. Waiters that skip hydration still re-verify the API key / steward JWT against the cached org/user. A revoked/re-scoped credential falls through to the authoritative gate.
- **Never hangs a turn**: `getOrSet` falls through to an independent hydration if the lock backend is absent or the holder is slow past the poll window — worst case degrades to today's stampede, never a deadlock.

## Tests

- New stampede regression: **N=8 concurrent cold callers hydrate exactly once** (`requireUserOrApiKeyWithOrgLookup` called 1x, scope populated 1x).
- All existing scope-cache tests green (16/16). One session-cache assertion updated to clear the mock after the populate call (the populate now runs one warm, strictly-safer revalidation via the single-flight path).

## Collision check
No open PR touches `resolve-shared-agent.ts` or the scope stampede (checked 2026-07-22). Branched off fresh `origin/develop` (`d7bb3703d`).

Receipt: `projects/eliza-fleet/CONTENTION-2026-07-22.md`

# Risks

Low. Backend-only, single file of behavior (`resolveSharedAgent`). The sliding refresh runs ONLY after the existing credential re-validation passes and is best-effort (`void cache.set`, catches its own error) — a failure only means the next turn re-hydrates. Absolute 5-min cap bounds agent-row staleness so a tier/row change self-heals even under a continuously active conversation. No auth outcome changes; a revoked/expired/re-scoped credential still falls through to the authoritative gate and is never refreshed.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Kills the **residual** chat cold stall left after #16743 (session-auth cold-scope cache) and #16763 (single-flight hydration): **30s absolute-TTL expiry between turns.** `cache.get` never refreshes the shared-agent scope entry on read, so a conversation idled past 30s — exactly demo Q&A pacing — re-pays the two cold Hyperdrive waves (user/org hydration + agent lookup, the felt 1–4.4s) on the *next* turn, and it recurs on every slow turn, not just the first.

Fix: on a validated direct-get hit, slide the entry's TTL back to 30s so an **active** conversation stays warm across human think-time, bounded by an absolute cap (`resolveMaxAgeMs` = 5 min from first write) so the cached agent row still self-heals. `firstWrittenAtMs` is stamped on every authoritative write and preserved across refreshes to enforce the cap. Sliding refresh is applied only on the direct-get hit — the single-flight populate/waiter branch already writes a fresh full-TTL entry, so the cold path does not double-write.

## What kind of change is this?

Improvement (latency) — non-breaking. No public API or schema change.

# Documentation changes needed?

No.

# Background

# Risks

Low-to-medium interaction risk. The change affects deliberate horizontal pager gestures and installed-shell `touch-pan-y` semantics. Vertical scrolling, notification expansion, composer focus, cold relaunch, and live Cloud chat were exercised on the physical LP3. The literal CSS rule is scoped to installed native/PWA shells and implements the semantics already requested by the existing utility class.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Keeps an ambiguous, slightly diagonal finger start pending until one axis clearly dominates.
- Commits a deliberate slow drag after 30% rather than 50% of the viewport while retaining the existing velocity flick path.
- Prevents native scrolling from taking over after horizontal intent locks.
- Pins installed-shell `.touch-pan-y` to literal `touch-action: pan-y` for older bundled WebViews.
- Adds unit, CSS-contract, and real CDP touch regression coverage. The CDP test uses a one-second 35%-width drag and fails against the old 50% hook.

## Root cause

The LP3 bundles Chrome WebView 113. Tailwind 4's generated custom-property `touch-action` form is accepted there but computes to `auto`; the browser claims the drag and emits `pointercancel` after roughly 8 CSS pixels. Desktop/mirror mouse drags therefore passed while a real finger moved the rail slightly and snapped it back.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix; no API, protocol, backend, auth, provisioning, or data-model changes.

# Documentation changes needed?

No user documentation change is required. Durable rationale is kept beside the pager constants and installed-shell CSS compatibility rule.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Start with `packages/ui/src/hooks/useHorizontalPager.ts`, then the installed-shell rule in `packages/ui/src/styles/styles.css` and the physical-device walkthrough below.

57:T97e,## What

Any in-process runtime re-bootstrap silently resurrected every operator-skipped plugin. Observed live: `@elizaos/plugin-browser` (skipped on a headless VPS because BROWSER death-loops with no display) came back mid-session and the death-loops returned with it.

## Why

The PGlite-recovery retry uses `ELIZA_SKIP_PLUGINS` as its scratch channel to skip crash-implicated plugins — and on every SUCCESSFUL bootstrap it ran `delete process.env.ELIZA_SKIP_PLUGINS`, destroying the operator's configured list. First boot honored it; any later bootstrap in the same process (config reload, recovery, restart-in-place) booted the full plugin set.

## How

`skip-plugins-env.ts`: capture the operator value at module load; on settle, restore it (never delete); on the recovery retry, merge operator + crash-implicated lists (deduped). Bug class: a user-facing env var used as internal mutable state.

## Evidence

Live before/after boot logs: pre-fix boot registers `plugin-browser`/`plugin-inbox`/`plugin-gitpathologist` despite all three in `ELIZA_SKIP_PLUGINS` (post-re-bootstrap); post-fix boot registers none of them, across repeated restarts.

- [x] `N/A - boot-path env handling; no UI surface. Evidence is the live boot-log before/after above.`
- Unit: 6/6 (merge dedupe, restore-vs-delete, absent-operator cases); app-core typecheck exits 0.

58:Td75,## Summary

Fixes #16827.

This keeps the LP3 physical-proof branch/APK frozen and puts the model-catalog compatibility repair in a separate branch from current `origin/develop`.

## Root cause

Read-only LP3 forensics showed the installed demo APK was healthy/authenticated and using the baked local renderer, but the chat renderer logged:

```text
[useSlashCommandController] Failed to load the model catalog; model completions will be empty TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading providers)
```

The live dedicated Cloud endpoint used by the LP3 answered `GET /api/models?catalogOnly=1` with top-level `providers` and no `catalog` object. The slash-command hook assumed only `models.catalog.providers`, so rolling/older cloud response shapes could throw before degrading model completions.

Endpoint evidence from the LP3 tenant:

```json
{"topKeys":["providers"],"hasCatalog":false,"providersTopLevelType":"dict","providersTopLevelCount":2}
```

## What changed

- Widens the UI DTO boundary for `ModelCatalogResponse` so response readers normalize/validate JSON instead of assuming the transport shape.
- Adds a guarded `resolveModelCatalogProviders` boundary in `useSlashCommandController`:
  - prefer current `catalog.providers`
  - accept top-level providers only when they have the curated catalog entry shape
  - degrade to no model completions for incompatible/legacy provider-model-list shapes instead of throwing or fabricating entries
- Adds regression coverage for both top-level curated catalog compatibility and legacy provider-list rejection.

## Validation

- `node packages/shared/scripts/generate-keywords.mjs`
- `bun run --cwd packages/ui test useSlashCommandController.models.test.ts` — 7 passed
- `bunx @biomejs/biome check packages/ui/src/api/client-types-core.ts packages/ui/src/chat/useSlashCommandController.ts packages/ui/src/chat/useSlashCommandController.models.test.ts`
- `bun run --cwd packages/ui typecheck`
- `git diff --check`

## Device/deploy safety

No device mutation, reinstall, route change, chat send, restart, or data clear was performed for this PR. #16822 / LP3 installed APK remains frozen at physical-proof SHA `31fe745039` and installed APK SHA `b16ca4ddd7b08d5d0e8070fa52517e5e4bde15f150ff63168c9a44559188dab1`.

# Risks

Low. The production voice path is behavior-identical: the cap constant moved from a private literal in `session.ts` to an exported constant on the Cartesia adapter, same value, same call site. The harness changes only make evidence runs measure what production actually does (cap passed; no teardown revoke). The ws-lifecycle change strengthens an assertion.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

**#16607 shipped the Cartesia buffer-delay cap to production but not to the evidence harness that certifies it.** Three parity gaps:

- **Reference server missed the cap** — `voice-session-server.ts` opened Cartesia with no `maxBufferDelayMs`, then streamed sentence-by-sentence with open contexts: exactly the pattern that stacks Cartesia's 3000ms default aggregation window. Latency evidence produced through it measured a configuration production no longer has. The cap is now `VOICE_TTS_MAX_BUFFER_DELAY_MS`, exported from the adapter both consumers already import, so the harness provably shares production's value instead of re-typing a literal that can drift.
- **The cap assertion under-enforced its own invariant** — the ws-lifecycle test filtered requests on `max_buffer_delay_ms !== undefined`, so a generation request that *dropped* the field vanished from the assertion instead of failing it. Requests are now selected positively (transcript-framed, excluding only the cancel frame) and every one must carry the cap. The same every-request invariant is pinned at adapter level with the exported constant.
- **The real-server harness still passed `onTeardownRevoke`** (#16663 low item) — production dropped the teardown revoke in #16636 (a successful hello already claims the jti until expiry). Evidence runs certified a denylist write production does not perform. Removed, with a harness test asserting the built session carries no teardown revoke and that the revocation poll stays wired through the real jwt module. Forwarding the harness's request-scoped store to the poll follows once #16669's signature change lands (noted in code).

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (evidence-harness parity with production; test-integrity strengthening).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

- `voice-session-server.integration.test.ts` — the full-turn case now asserts the reference server opens Cartesia with `VOICE_TTS_MAX_BUFFER_DELAY_MS` (captured stream options, compared against the real adapter export).
- `cartesia-sonic-tts.test.ts` — "the exported production cap rides on EVERY generation request": three streamed clauses (continuation + terminal), positively selected, each must carry the cap.
- `ws-lifecycle.test.ts` — the production-path assertion now fails if any transcript-framed request omits the cap, instead of silently excluding it.
- `harness-real-server.test.ts` — asserts no `onTeardownRevoke` in the built session config and the revocation poll wiring.

## Detailed testing steps

None: automated tests are acceptable. Composed changed-test lane (CI-pinned bun 1.3.14, `--conditions=eliza-source`): **56 pass / 0 fail** across the three composable suites + **1 pass** for the process-isolated harness suite. Changed-file line coverage from those lanes: `cartesia-sonic-tts.ts` 99.8%, `session.ts` 91.3%, `voice-session-server.ts` 90.8%, `harness-real-server.ts` 63.6% — all above the 50% floor.

5b:T9f5,## Summary
Fixes the **Server Tests** lane (`packages/agent`), red on `develop` since 07-19 with:

```
Cannot find package '@octokit/core' imported from
plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/node_modules/@octokit/rest/dist-src/index.js
 ❯ ../../plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/dist/node/index.node.js:242
```

The whole `proactive-interaction-pipeline.test.ts` suite failed to collect (0 tests), failing `bun run test:server`.

## Root cause
`workspace-service.ts` / `workspace-github.ts` statically `import { Octokit } from "@octokit/rest"`. That import is pulled into the agent test graph via `server-helpers-swarm.ts` (`sanitizeCompletionRelay`). `@octokit/rest@22` is nested in the plugin's own `node_modules` (a version conflict with `git-workspace-service` / `lerna`, both on `@octokit/rest@20`, keeps `@22` off the hoist root). Its transitive graph (`@octokit/core`, `@octokit/request`, …) lives only in the bun store and is reachable purely via realpath. Node's native resolver walks realpath and finds them, but Vite/vitest runs with `preserveSymlinks: true` and resolves the transitives eagerly at module-load, where they are **not** visible — so the suite dies at collection.

Introduced when the GitHub workspace provider was wired in (#16576 / #16453 era), which made the octokit import load-bearing in the agent test chain.

## Fix
Load `@octokit/rest` lazily via `createRequire` — the exact pattern these files already use for `git-workspace-service` — and defer the require to first use inside `createGitHubRequest`. The GitHub provider is an optional integration; nothing needs octokit at module load. This removes the top-level ESM import the symlink-preserving resolver choked on, while runtime behavior is identical (Node's `require` resolves the store realpath correctly).

- No dependency added/changed, no `bun.lock` churn.
- No workflow edits, no test weakening, no skips.

## Verification
- `packages/agent` `proactive-interaction-pipeline.test.ts`: **9/9** (was a failed suite / 0 tests).
- Full `@elizaos/plugin-agent-orchestrator` suite (exercises the GitHub provider / octokit path): **2522 passed, 7 skipped, 0 failed** (222 files).
- Built dist confirmed: 0 top-level `import … "@octokit/rest"`, 2 lazy `createRequire("@octokit/rest")`.

## Scope note
The other Server Tests app-core reds (plugin-path boundary, 503, ambient-cloud-key) are owned by #16688 and are intentionally **not** touched here. This PR is the missing octokit piece #16688 does not address.

Co-authored-by: wakesync <shadow@shad0w.xyz>

For example, this switch now reports the missing <code>2n</code> case:</p>

<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/10972">#10972</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/ab8c21b35e81708276e4283a4a0ff86ea815e345"><code>ab8c21b</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/ematipico"><code>@​ematipico</code></a>! - Fixed <a href="https://biomejs.dev/linter/rules/use-exhaustive-switch-cases/"><code>useExhaustiveSwitchCases</code></a> for unions of bigint literals. The rule now reports missing bigint cases and compares bigint literals by value, including binary, octal, hexadecimal, and separator-containing spellings. For example, this switch now reports the missing <code>2n</code> case:</p>
<pre lang="ts"><code>declare const value: 1n | 2n;
switch (value) {
  case 1n:
    break;
}
</code></pre>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/10972">#10972</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/ab8c21b35e81708276e4283a4a0ff86ea815e345"><code>ab8c21b</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/ematipico"><code>@​ematipico</code></a>! - Fixed false positives in <a href="https://biomejs.dev/linter/rules/no-base-to-string/"><code>noBaseToString</code></a> and <a href="https://biomejs.dev/linter/rules/use-nullish-coalescing/"><code>useNullishCoalescing</code></a> when member, stringification, or nullish inference cannot complete. These rules now suppress diagnostics instead of reporting from partial type information. For example, neither expression is reported when a recursive type cannot be fully resolved:</p>
<pre lang="ts"><code>type Recursive = Recursive;
declare const value: Recursive;
<p>String(value);<br />
value || &quot;fallback&quot;;<br />
</code></pre></p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/10977">#10977</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/0bf748653e488d0b959d39847641438cdb28188b"><code>0bf7486</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/ematipico"><code>@​ematipico</code></a>! - Fixed <a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/issues/10922">#10922</a>: the action <a href="https://biomejs.dev/assist/actions/use-sorted-attributes/"><code>useSortedAttributes</code></a> no longer triggers for HTML instructions.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/10957">#10957</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/cf263c4700e9f24115e541d1f142934a9b2d878f"><code>cf263c4</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/dyc3"><code>@​dyc3</code></a>! - Fixed <a href="https://biomejs.dev/linter/rules/no-then-property/"><code>noThenProperty</code></a> failing to detect <code>Object.fromEntries</code>, <code>Object.defineProperty</code>, and <code>Reflect.defineProperty</code> calls with comments between their tokens.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/10983">#10983</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/edc0ed738ab8da3d512d527196684bd668090854"><code>edc0ed7</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/ayaangazali"><code>@​ayaangazali</code></a>! - Fixed <a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/issues/10980">#10980</a>: <a href="https://biomejs.dev/linter/rules/use-aria-props-supported-by-role/"><code>useAriaPropsSupportedByRole</code></a> no longer reports false positives when the attribute that determines an element's implicit ARIA role is written as a shorthand attribute, such as <code>&lt;a {href} aria-label=&quot;...&quot;&gt;</code> in Astro and Svelte files.</p>
<p>Shorthand attributes are now taken into account when computing the implicit role, so the anchor above correctly resolves to the <code>link</code> role instead of <code>generic</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/10889">#10889</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/89526e3858c437408ec9ff192c35a866ad991d1b"><code>89526e3</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/denbezrukov"><code>@​denbezrukov</code></a>! - Fixed CSS formatter casing for syntax-owned names while preserving author-defined names, including scoped keyframes and container scroll-state queries.</p>
<pre lang="diff"><code>- A:HOVER { COLOR: INITIAL; }
+ A:hover { color: initial; }
- @KEYFRAMES :GLOBAL KeepFrames { FROM { COLOR: RED; } }
+ @keyframes :GLOBAL KeepFrames { from { color: RED; } }
- @CONTAINER scroll-state((SCROLLED: TOP) AND (STUCK)) { A:HOVER { COLOR: RED; } }
+ @container scroll-state((SCROLLED: TOP) AND (STUCK)) { A:hover { color: RED; } }
</code></pre>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/biomejs/biome/pull/10964">#10964</a> <a href="https://github.com/biomejs/biome/commit/794ccd0528345c4eaa87af1d86f02475277a0a22"><code>794ccd0</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/denbezrukov"><code>@​denbezrukov</code></a>! - Fixed CSS formatting for comments between declaration values and <code>!important</code>.</p>
<pre lang="diff"><code>-a { color: /* before */ /* after */ red !important; }
+a { color: /* before */ red /* after */ !important; }
</code></pre>
</li>
</ul>
